景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

～「良い金利上昇」を実現するための4つの論点～

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

Document 027: 2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略より21 世紀ニッポンの「 ア
ポロ計画」を 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 202 6 年 10 月 27 日

心情的にはノーサレンダー路線 
先月の論考では、①度重なる成長戦略にもかかわらず日本の潜在成長率は低下したこと、②成長戦略
には政策手段の特定が困難という基本問題があるこ と 、③25 年英知を尽くしても結果が出なかった事実
は厳粛に受け止めるべきこと、などを述べた 1。 
それを踏まえ、本稿ではあるべき方向性を考えてみたい。選択肢は、(a)25 年で諦めず50 年でも100
年でもやる、(b)成長目標を達成しやすい水準に下げる、(c)政府のミッションは成長戦略とは別にある
と考える、の 3 つである。選択肢(b)が無難に見えるが、もともと成長戦略は「現状の成長率ではだめ
だ」という認識に基づくものである。現状を明確に上回る成長率を目指さない限り、成長戦略としては
自己矛盾である。 
すると残るのは (a)ノーサレンダー路線、(c)ミッション再考、 のいずれかである。このうちノーサレ
ンダー路線、つまり「成長を諦めない」という不屈の姿勢には人々の共感が得られやすい。しかし、不
屈の志を掲げても、手段の裏付けを欠くという現実は変わらない。手段の裏付けを欠くなら「目標」で
はなく「夢」である。もちろん、夢を語ることが悪いわけではないし、政治家は夢を語るべきという考
えもあるだろう。その夢が偶然にでもかなえば歴史に残る偉業であり、夢なら実現せずとも落胆の度合
いは小さい。ノーサレンダー路線はアップサイドだけでダウンサイドはない、と考えてよいだろうか。 
成長戦略のダウンサイド 
成長戦略にダウンサイドはない、やってみて損はない、と い う前提は実は正しくない。成長戦略は「た
だ」でできるわけではないからだ。例えば、成長戦略には多かれ少なかれネット財政支出が伴う。国債
をどれだけ発行できるのか議論の余地はあるが、いずれにせよ有限である。 
一方、成長戦略は総花的になりやすい。成長を実現する手段がはっきりしない以上、ある政策案を「成
長とは無関係」ときっぱり排除するのも難しいからである。経済界の要望や政治家の信念に基づく様々
な政策案が、成長に少しでも関係しそうなら幅広く正当化される。各種施策の費用対効果について一定

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「成長戦略の本質的な限界」 （2026年 9 月25 日 ）。

### Page 2

2

の分析や議論は行われている。しかし、行われることとそれが有効に機能することは別である。費用対
効果の分析が有効に機能し、各種施策の経済成長へのインパクトが正確に測れていたとしたら、25 年に
わたり成長目標の大幅未達という結果にはならなかったはずである。 
一般に、政策目標の実現に向けてリソースが投入され、目標が実現できなかった場合、そのリソース
投入は失敗だったことになる。チャレンジ自体は無駄ではなかったと言える場合もあるが、過去 25 年
だめだった目標にもう一度挑戦してみるという話なら、たぶん次もだめだろうと事前に予想できる。金
も人もふんだんにあるなら別だが、財政状況が厳しく、人材の不足も叫ばれる国で、高確率で失敗する
ミッションへのリソース投入は賢明な選択と言えるのか。 
有限のリソースが無駄に使われれば、他の課題のために使えるリソースが減る。ここで言うリソース
は財政資金だけではない。実現できない目的のために、多忙な官僚の労力が割かれ、政治家のポリティ
カル ・ キャピタルも消耗すれば、やればできたはずの他の課題への対応まで中途半端に終わってしまう。
成長戦略には他の課題を犠牲にするという「機会費用」が伴うのであり、それが「ただではない」とい
うことのより正確な意味である。「成長を諦めないノーサレンダー路線でいく」という選択は、「50 年で
も 100 年でもその機会費用を払い続けることを覚悟する」という選択でもある。 
何のための経済成長か 
ここで話を一段階前に戻してみよう。そもそもなぜ、経済成長を高めるべきと考えられているのだろ
うか。もちろん、低成長より高成長の方がいいに決まっているので、経済成長を簡単に高められるので
あれば、そんな疑問を持つ必要はない。しかし、経済成長を高めようとすることで機会費用を払い続け
ることになる確率が高いなら、そうまでしてわれわれが最終的に望んでいるものは何なのかという問い
に、きちんと向き合う必要がある。 
経済成長の必要性を訴える主張は、「〇〇の解決には経済成長が不可欠」「低成長のままなら〇〇のよ
うな不都合が起きる」というパターンにおおむね当てはまるように思う。つまり、現在直面している課
題、あるいは将来直面しそうな課題に対応するには、より高い経済成長が必要という認識が成長戦略の
基礎にあると考えられる。念頭に置かれている課題の中身は、社会保障や財政の持続性、行政・医療・
介護サービスの維持、国際的な存在感の回復、生活水準の向上など、論者により様々である。パイを増
やせるなら改善できる課題は多いので、経済成長を高めるべきという主張は万人受けし、政治家には格
好の政策アジェンダになる。 
しかし、「何らかの課題を経済成長で解決する」という考え方は 、「その課題に直接向き合うより経済
成長を高める方が簡単だ」という前提がないと成立しない。その前提が間違っていれば、むしろ経済成
長に頼るがゆえに肝心の課題がいつまでも解決しないことになる。重要な課題であればあるほど、経済
成長と切り離して解決する努力を早く始めるべきではないか。 
例えば、社会保障や財政の持続性が課題なのであれば、給付と負担のバランスや、恵まれている人と
そうでない人の公平感を改善することで、問題の解決を目指す方が良い。人々の生活水準を高めたいと
いう課題を経済成長と切り離すのは少し厄介だが、国民全員が等しく困っているわけではなく、問題の

### Page 3

3

本質は経済格差にある。分配政策により、国民生活の満足感や社会の安定性を全体として改善すること
はできる。 
国際社会における日本の存在感を高めることは、経済成長ではそもそも無理であろう。かつて日本が
世界第2 位のGDP をキープし、経済大国として存在感を示した時代もあった。しかし、あれは冷戦構造
のもとでの日米関係、戦後の高度成長とその余熱、新興国が発展初期段階にあったことなどによる歴史
の偶然であった。GDP の対世界シェアが3～4％程度まで落ち、今後さらに人口が減り、資源・エネルギ
ーに乏しく、特殊な言語を使う島国が、今から経済成長を多少高められたとしても、そのことで示せる
国際パワーは高が知れている。小さくても存在感がある国をどう作るかが、正しいアジェンダ設定だと
思われる。 
21 世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」は？ 
日本が課題だらけなのは確かだが、25 年間どうやっても達成できなかった経済成長に、その解決をこ
れ以上頼るのが正解とは思いにくい。逆に、優先課題の解決に国が本気でリソースを投入するなら、そ
こに民間も収益機会を見出し、様々な連鎖反応を通じて経済に好影響が及ぶ可能性も出てくる。 
経済学者マリアナ・マッツカートは著書「ミッション・エコノミー」において、大きな変革は市場メ
カニズムだけでは生み出せず、国家が野心的だが実行可能な課題を設定し、民間の力を引き出す仕組み
を築くことが重要と説く 2。マッツカートは代表例として、1960 年代の米国アポロ計画を挙げる。1969
年 7 月 20 日、 ニール・アームストロングとバズ・オルドリンが月面に降り立ったことで知られる、あの
アポロ計画である。 
日本の成長戦略も「野心的な課題」を掲げたものではあるが、複雑すぎるマクロ経済そのものを課題
の対象にすると、先月の論考で詳しく述べたように、目的と手段の関係性が特定できない。原因と結果
の関係がよくわからない仕事は、責任の所在があいまいで互いに人任せになり、結果にコミットするこ
との効果が得られない。それに対し、「月に人を送る」などのより具体的なミッションの場合、そのため
には何が必要なのかの因果関係を分析・認識しやすい分、結果にコミットすれば人々の力が正しい方向
へ向かう。 
アポロ計画は、半導体、コンピューター、通信、材料工学などの技術を飛躍的に発展させ、その後の
米国の産業基盤を築いたと言われている。また、計画推進の国家機関であった米航空宇宙局 （NASA）は 、
多くの科学技術人材を育成・輩出した。彼らはその後、金融イノベーションなどでも活躍し、NASA 出身
なので「ロケット・サイエンティスト」と呼ばれたことはよく知られている。 
ここで重要なのは、半導体やコンピューターなどの産業育成自体を目的として、政府が補助金で支援
したという話ではないことである。アポロ計画という具体的なミッションがまず存在し、それへの貢献
に適正な対価で報いる仕組みがイノベーターたちを突き動かし、様々な技術や知見が開発、蓄積された
のである。後世に残った産業基盤や人材は、「月に人を送る」というミッションの副産物であった。

2 Mazzucato, Mariana(2021) Mission Economy: A Moonshot Guide to Changing Capitalism, Allen Lane-Penguin 
（関美和・鈴木絵里子訳『ミッション・エコノミー』News Picks、2021 年 ）。

### Page 4

4

一般論として、何らかの課題を解決すれば、経済に必ず大きなプラスの効果が見込める、とまでは言
えない。しかし、上位目標が「課題の解決」なのだから、それが達成された時点でミッションは成功で
あり、投入されたリソースは「有効に使われた」ことになる。そのうえで、マクロ経済にもプラスの波
及効果があればなお良いが、仮にそれがなくても課題が解決された分、以前より望ましい社会になって
いる。 
最大の難関は、優先課題を特定しそれに取り組む社会的モメンタムを生み出すことであり、それには
既存の発想に捉われない強力なビジョンとリーダーシップが要る。アポロ計画にも当初「無謀で財政資
金の無駄使い」という声があった。マッツカートは先ほどの著書の中で、仮に現代風の費用対効果分析
を適用していたら、アポロ計画は採用されず、人類が月面に立つことはなかったと言っている。 
アポロ計画に相当する案件は、どの時代、どの国にも自明なものとしてあるわけではない。 「月に人を
送る」というミッションは、当時の米ソ冷戦状況において、ソ連に宇宙開発で打ち勝つことが米国の死
活問題、という歴史特殊的な認識のもとで生まれた。それでも、よく考えてみれば今の日本にも、人口
の減少 ・ 高齢化や地政学環境の激変など、 人類史上にもまれな歴史特殊的な条件のもとで、 「死活問題」
になりつつある課題はいくつかあるように思う。例えば、エネルギーや食料の安定供給、生活インフラ ・
医療・介護体制の維持・改善などは、次世代の日本人にとってまさに「死活問題」なのではないか。 
限られたリソースの優先課題への配分が、市場メカニズムで実現できないなら、国が強力に関与しな
ければならない。民間には不得意な国民的課題の解決こそ、国の本来の役割であり、そのためのリソー
スを国債発行でまかないきれないなら、国民に追加負担を求めてもいい。 
言うまでもなくこれは困難な作業である。だから優先課題は何か、そのために国民負担を求めるべき
かなどを深く議論せず、経済成長で様々な問題をまとめて解決できることにしておく方が、実はずっと
楽なのである。しかし、それが問題の先送りにすぎないことは繰り返し述べたとおりである。今も成長
戦略が重要だと言い、政府にそれを求めたがるわれわれの意識は、日本が直面する真の課題やその優先
順位を考えるのは面倒だ、 という思考停止から生まれているのかもしれない。21 世紀ニッポンの「アポ
ロ計画」は何なのか、国民的な議論の活性化が必要ではないだろうか。

Unimicron (3037.TW)
28 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Long-term strategies: Resources concentrated on ABF/ AI PCB — As we
highlighted, Unimicron has been converting BT capacity into ABF, which currently
causes some BT capacity to be idle during the transition period (negative impact will
likely last into 1H27, in our view). Thus, currently, we are less bullish on its BT GM
recovery in the near term vs other BT peers. However, we expect this conversion will

help the company to secure more ABF demand earlier considering customers
currently are still waiting for those ABF greenfield capacity expansion projects to
come in the next few years. For HDI/mSAP , Unimicron would only focus on AI
server/optical module products in the long term. To note that, the company is still
less aggressive in HDI capacity expansion given higher competition from Chinese
peers. Meanwhile, the company is also downsizing its traditional PCB capacity (i.e.,

for non-AI business) and just announced its plan to sell its PCB plant in Japan to
other players. We expect there will be more downsizing plans to come for traditional
PCB capacity.
GM expansion on track; reiterate Buy — We raise our 2026/27 /28E by 10%/5%/5%
to factor in robust GM expansion by ABF. We remain positive on its GM expansion by
ABF and also see potential upside coming from HDI and BT segments. We raise our
DCF-based TP to NT$1,550 (30x 2027E EPS) from NT$1,500 and reiterate our Buy

rating.

### Page 6

Unimicron (3037.TW)
28 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Unimicron
Company description
Established in 1990, Unimicron is the world's No.1 PCB maker and HDI board
maker. After the acquisition of PPT in late 2009, its revenue exposure to
substrate has surpassed 40%, with the rest almost equally shared by
conventional PCB and HDI. Unimicron has manufacturing sites in Taiwan and
China (Suzhou, Kunshan, Shenzhen). UMC is the largest shareholder, with a
~13% stake.

Investment strategy
We rate Unimicron shares a Buy. Our positive view is premised on: more
upside potential in the ABF segment thanks to product-mix improvements,
better pricing on more complex product upgrade, and capacity expansion.
Despite macro uncertainty, from a long-term perspective, we believe the
tightness in ABF is unlikely to ease without much new capacity ramped
aggressively in the upcoming years, and Unimicron should be the largest
beneficiary with its scale in ABF. Thanks to AI GPU/ASIC demand, we now see

strong growth in 2026/27E ABF consumption. We believe Unimicron could
see strong revenue growth again as it did in 2019–21 from both ABF new
capacity and a BT rebound.
Valuation
Our target price for Unimicron is NT$1,550. We use a 1-year forward-looking
DCF-based valuation (ie, end-2027) to reflect Unimicron's ability to generate
stable cash. With a risk-free rate of 5.0% (10-year government bond rate), a
market risk premium of 7%, and an equity beta of 1.43 (2-year weekly share

price), we calculate Unimicron's WACC as 14.6%. Our target price is equivalent
to 62x/30x 2026/27E EPS, which is higher than the +1std level of consensus
PE multiple of the past five years. However, as the market focuses on 2027
upcycle, we believe valuation on 2027E EPS is still in line with historical peak
valuations of 25–30x during the last upcycles.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean Unimicron shares trade below our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected HDI business; 2) significant increase

in ABF supply in the industry; and 3) stronger-than-expected margin pressure
from depreciation due to higher capex investments.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

Powertech Technology

Up-cycle level of earnings to continue; 
reiterate Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 500.00 TWD | Price: 254.50 TWD

Reiterate Buy on above-trend growth and margins 
We believe Powertech Technology Inc. (PTI) can continue to benefit from the AI boom 
and an upturn in the memory chip industry. 3Q results clearly confirm record-high 
revenue (NT$25bn; up 27% YoY) and an above-trend gross margin (24%; normal high 
teen% or 20% range). 4Q guidance (for packaging/testing businesses) also remained 
bullish: DRAM – tight supply vs strong demand; NAND – order increase for more value-
added NAND and enterprise SSD; and logic – mix improvement with FC-BGA and price 
hike (following cost increase). Key customers are Micron, Kioxia, Solidigm (owned by SK 
Hynix) and various Greater China logic chip makers that require outsourced packaging 
and testing services. As we have already assumed up-cycle earnings level for 2027-28, 
our EPS estimates remain largely unchanged. We maintain our PO of NT$500 (27x 2027-
28E P/E applied). Reiterate Buy.  
Key takeaways from 3Q results  
Five takeaways from 3Q results: (1) strong execution in newly growing packaging orders 
for high-end memory (low-power / high-speed DRAM, enterprise SSD, 200+ layers 
NAND), (2) increasing exposure to more advanced logic (through FC-BGA, etc), (3) a well-
balanced sales mix (DRAM: 20% of total sales; NAND: 28%; SiP/modules 9%; logic 43%), 
(4) notably improved gross margins (limited increase in manufacturing costs vs strong 
top-line growth seen), and (5) solid FCF generation (EBITDA: c.NT$8bn vs capex: 
NT$5bn; expected annual dividend yield: 3-4% range).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 15.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Headquartered in Taiwan, PTI is one of the largest OSAT 
firms, which packages and tests memory and logic chips. It 
has around 18,000 employees. The logic chip-related 
business operates under Greatek Electronics (fully 
consolidated into earnings). Management's new focus areas 
in packaging/testing are PLP, TSV, 3D NAND, low-power 
DRAM, SSD, etc.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on strong earnings growth, driven 
by more value-added memory and even logic packaging 
solutions. 1Q-3Q26 earnings have already hit upcycle levels, 
posting a strong YoY growth (largely driven by NAND/SSD). 
We believe the memory super-cycle and customers' 
ongoing outsourcing trend (Micron, Solidigm/Hynix, Kioxia 
and Greater China's logic chipmakers) using Powertech's 
proven backend solutions should be the key contributors to 
strong earnings growth by 2028.

Powertech’s recent news summary during Jul-Aug 2026 
Date Key news Summary 
Aug-2026 FOPLP 
production 
-Powertech plans to start production at the world's first panel-level chip packaging facility for AI chips as soon as early 2027 to make server CPUs, AI 
computing chips and advanced optics 
-Powertech said its advanced FOPLP production line has secured a 100% UTR through 2030 ahead of its scheduled volume productio n in 2027 
-Powertech plans to invest NT$70bn (US$2.2bn) in its FOPLP facility, with installed capacity set to increase from 1,000 panels per months in 2026 
to 5,000 in 2028 
Jul-2026 JV with Broadcom -Powertech reported plans to invest US$400mn to create a new JV with Broadcom in Singapore to cater to customer demand for advanced panel-
based packaging services 
-The JV will concentrate on offering additive finer-geometry redistribution layer (RDL) tech for advanced package substrates  
 AMD business -Powertech reaffirmed that it is on track to start shipping AI chips utilizing its advanced PLP tech for AMD in 2H27, dismissing concern s over delays 
-AMD will be one of Powertech's first customers adopting the FOPLP tech, which is considered to be more cost-effective than the mainstream 
CoWoS tech 
  Capex/capacity -Powertech plans to acquire the entire Philippine unit of ON Semiconductor for US$45mn as part of its efforts to navigate global supply chain 
realignment amid escalating geopolitical risks 
-The transaction will be conducted by its subsidiary, Greatek Electronics in which Powertech holds 43% stake

### Page 13

Price objective basis & risk 
Powertech Technology (PWOGF) 
Our PO of NT$500 is derived from 27x P/E based on 2027-28E EPS. This is 10-20% 
higher than 2024-25 peak levels (low- to mid-20x). Since we expect 50%+ higher EPS in 
2026-28E vs 2024-25 on the back of record-high sales/margin, we believe fair multiples 
should be at least on par with the previous peak level. True, the long-term historical 
average P/E came in the range of 10-20x, but the memory super-cycle and AI boom 
should warrant above-trend earnings growth and consequently over 20x fair P/E. 
Taiwan's OSAT stocks also trade at 20-30x P/E.

Upside risk is mostly related to strong packaging orders from memory chipmakers and 
even logic chip suppliers.

Downside risk is mostly associated with an unexpected memory industry downturn or AI 
bubble (e.g., significant packaging order cut).

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異 
營收 1,630 1,613 +1% 
稅前淨利 394 417 -5.5% 
稅後淨利 326 333 -2.2% 
EPS(元) 2.28 2.34 -2.6%

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(L TA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 L TA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 L TA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 L TA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우)

합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 09 월 30일)

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

Document 279: AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/6 
AI 関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか 
～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 世界的な AI ブームが続くなか、AI 関連企業がデータセンター建設などの巨額投資を賄うた
めに社債発行や借入による資金調達を急増させている。「AI関連企業は手元資金が潤沢で財
務が盤石だ」という従来の常識は揺らぎつつあり、企業の資金不足や信用リスクに目を向け
る必要がある。 
○ AI 投資の拡大は3 つの変化を通じて、企業の財務と金融市場にリスクをもたらしうる。第一
に、テクノロジー企業の社債発行が急増し、借入依存が強まっている。第二に、巨額の設備
投資がAI大手の現金創出力（フリーキャッシュフロー）を圧迫している。第三に、信用力の
低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒が強まり、信用スプレッドが拡大している。 
○ 「AIの成長期待さえあれば巨額の投資もすべて回収できる」と安易に想定すべきではない。
AI 投資の資金を借入などの外部資金に頼る度合いが高まる環境では、収益化の遅れが企業の
信用力低下に直結しやすく、従来の資産配分についても再点検が必要となる。 
○ 個人投資家にとっては、保有する社債ファンドの業種偏りや信用リスクを点検することと、
株式運用において特定の AI 銘柄への過度な集中を避け、安定したキャッシュフローを生む
資産を補完的に組み入れることが、対応策の一つとなるだろう。

1. 「キャッシュリッチなAI企業」の変調 
生成AIの普及を原動力とする世界的なハイテク株高は、日本の個人投資家にとっても資産形成
の大きな追い風となってきた。NISA（少額投資非課税制度）などを通じて米国株式（S&P500な
ど）や全世界株式を保有する投資家の多くは、AI関連企業の成長や株価上昇の恩恵を受けてきた
といえる。もっとも、足元ではAI関連株の値動きが荒くなる場面も増え、巨額の投資が本当に利
益につながるのか不安を感じている投資家も少なくないだろう。一方で、市場には依然として「A
I企業の財務は盤石であり、足元の揺らぎは一時的な調整にすぎない」とみる楽観論も根強い。し
かし、企業の「台所事情」に目を向けると、これまでとは異なるリスクが見えてくる。 「AIブー
ム」と聞くと、華やかな技術革新や株価の上昇ばかりに目が向きがちだが、企業財務の視点に立
つと、巨額のデータセンター投資が企業の手元資金を圧迫し、一部のAI関連企業では借入などの

### Page 2

2/6 
外部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
華やかなAI株高の裏側で膨らむ巨額の借金を、AI関連企業は本当に返せるのだろうか。 「テクノ
ロジー企業は潤沢な現金を持ち、財務が健全だ」という従来の常識にとらわれていると、資金不
足や信用リスクの拡大という構造的な変化を見落としかねない。本稿では、AI投資の拡大が引き
起こす 3つの変化として、 「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用スプレッドの拡大」を整
理し、個人投資家が資産運用において注意すべき論点を提示したい。なお、本稿では、AI投資を
主導する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）から、AI関連事業を手掛ける中堅企業ま
でを幅広く「AI関連企業」と総称する。また、 「テクノロジー企業」は、社債発行統計などにおい
てテクノロジー（IT）部門に分類される企業を指す用語として用いる。

2. AI投資の拡大はなぜ資金不足と信用リスクを招くのか 
(1) AI投資の拡大で急増するテクノロジー企業の社債発行 
現在のAIブームを牽引する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）をはじめとするAI関
連企業は、データセンターの建設や先端半導体の確保、電力インフラの整備などに巨額の資金を
投じている。これまでテクノロジー企業は、株式発行や潤沢な手元資金を主な資金源としてきた
が、投資規模の急拡大に伴い、資金調達のあり方が大きく変わりつつある。 
RBA（豪州準備銀行）が2026 年10月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」によれば、AI
関連企業は大規模な投資計画を賄うため、社債やプライベート・クレジット、銀行借入など、負
債による資金調達への依存を強めている。資料1の上段は米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占め
るテクノロジー部門の割合を、下段はハイパースケーラー（巨大クラウド・AI企業）とテクノロ
ジー部門の実際の社債発行額を示したものである。2026年には、米国の投資適格社債の発行総額
に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が約27％に達している。また、下段が示すように、足元ではハ
イパースケーラーによる起債が急増し、部門全体の社債発行額を大きく押し上げている。RBAは、
今後計画されているAI関連の設備投資のうち3分の1以上が負債性資金によって賄われるとの市
場の見方を紹介している。つまり、AIブームが続くほど、テクノロジー企業の借入依存が強ま
り、公募・私募の債券市場全体がAI投資の成否に左右されやすくなるという新たな構図が生まれ
ている。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 テクノロジー部門の社債発行が急増、米国投資適格社債の発行総額に占める割合は約 27％に 
（注）米国の非金融企業による投資適格社債の発行額（期間は 2010 年 3 月～2026 年 7 月 17 日）。テクノロジー部門にはコンピ
ュータ・電子機器を含む。上段の割合は 4 四半期移動平均。ハイパースケーラーは Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle、NVIDIA を指す。 
（出所）Reserve Bank of Australia, “Financial Stability Review – October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 巨額の設備投資が企業の現金創出力を圧迫 
なぜ、高い収益力を誇る巨大テクノロジー企業までもが借入への依存を強めているのか。その
背景には、データセンター構築などの設備投資があまりにも巨額であり、本業の稼ぐ力（営業利
益）だけでは投資資金を賄いきれなくなっている現実がある。 
IMF（国際通貨基金）が2026 年10月に公表した「Global Financial Stability Report」は、
AI投資の中心を担う主要ハイパースケーラー5社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle）の財務状況を分析している。資料2は、これら 5社の設備投資（Capex：Capital 
Expenditure） 、営業利益、およびフリーキャッシュフローの推移を示したものである。5社の設備
投資は 2023年頃から急拡大し、2025年後半から2026年にかけて営業利益を上回る規模に達して
いる。その結果、フリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）は急減し、2023～24年のピーク時には四半期
ベースで600 億米ドルを超えていたものが、2026年にはほぼゼロまで落ち込んでいる。もっと
も、ハイパースケーラー各社は現時点では強固なバランスシートと十分な流動性を維持してい
る。しかし、激しい競争圧力や技術の陳腐化に対応するため、企業が投資の手を緩めることは難
しい。今後、AIの収益化が市場の期待を下回るようなことがあれば、企業の現金創出力は一段と
圧迫され、追加の資金調達や財務負担の増大を招く可能性がある。

### Page 4

4/6

資料 2 主要ハイパースケーラー5 社の設備投資が急増し、2026 年のフリーキャッシュフローはほぼゼロに 
（注）主要ハイパースケーラー5 社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracle）の設備投資（Capex、左軸）、営業利益（左軸）、
およびフリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）の四半期ごとの推移（単位：10 億米ドル）。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒 
さらに、AI投資への借入依存が高まるなか、社債市場ではテクノロジー企業の信用リスクに対
する警戒が強まりつつある。株式市場ではAIの成長期待が注目されやすいが、元利金の確実な返
済を重視する債券市場では、企業の財務体力やビジネスモデルの持続可能性に対する選別がシビ
アに進んでいる。 
ECB（欧州中央銀行）が2026 年5月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」では、AIを巡
る懸念が株式市場だけでなく社債市場にも波及していると指摘している。資料3 は、2025年 3月
から 2026年5 月にかけてのユーロ圏および米国におけるハイイールド（HY、低格付け）社債の信
用スプレッドの推移を示したものである。米国・ユーロ圏ともに、ハイイールド債市場全体（米
国HY、ユーロ圏HY）のスプレッドが300ベーシスポイント（bp）前後で比較的落ち着いて推移し
ていたのに対し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業（米国HYテック、ユーロ圏HYテック）のスプレ
ッドは 2026年に入り500bp超へと大きく拡大し、同年 5月時点でも450bp前後と、市場全体を上
回る水準で推移していた。 
ECBは、AIが既存のソフトウェア企業などのビジネスモデルを脅かすリスクに加え、内部資金だ
けでなく借入に頼ってAI投資を拡大させる動きが金融安定上の懸念になりうると指摘している。
巨額のAI投資ブームの裏側では、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する市場の警戒が強まり、
企業の借入依存が信用市場にも新たなリスクをもたらしつつある。 「借りた巨額の資金を本当に返

### Page 5

5/6 
せるのか」という市場の問いかけがすでに始まっているのである。

資料 3 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業の信用スプレッドはハイイールド債市場全体より大きく拡大 
（注）2025 年 3 月 1 日～2026 年 5 月 19 日におけるユーロ圏および米国のハイイールド（HY）社債の信用スプレッド（OIS カーブ 
に対する上乗せ金利、時価総額加重平均、単位：ベーシスポイント）。「HY テック」はハイイールド格付けのテクノロジー企業
を指す。 
縦の破線は左から 2025 年 4 月の米国の関税発表、前回 FSR（金融安定報告書）公表時点（2025 年 11 月）、中東戦争の開 
始（2026 年 3 月初旬）を示す。 
（出所）European Central Bank, “Financial Stability Review, May 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 「AI借金時代」の資産防衛術 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI ブームの裏側では「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用
スプレッドの拡大」という3 つの変化が同時に進行している。この視点に立てば、AIブームが抱
える死角は「株価の割高感」だけでは説明できない。巨大テクノロジー企業から中堅のテクノロ
ジー企業に至るまで、AI投資の拡大に伴い、一部の企業では自己資金だけでなく、借入などの外
部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
ここで重要なのは、 「テクノロジー企業はキャッシュリッチで財務が盤石だ」と安易に考えない
ことである。仮にAI の収益化が遅れれば、膨らんだ借入負担が企業の業績や信用力を直撃するシ
ナリオも視野に入れる必要がある。こうした「AI借金時代」を迎えるなか、個人投資家には、従
来のポートフォリオに潜む信用リスクを改めて点検することが求められる。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
第一に、社債ファンドやハイイールド債（低格付け社債）における「信用リスク」を過小評価
しないことである。2026年の米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が
約27％に達し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業のスプレッドが拡大している現状では、 「債券だか
ら安心」 「株安を社債がカバーしてくれる」という従来の前提が働きにくい可能性がある。AI の収
益期待が後退する局面では、AI 関連株の下落と信用スプレッドの拡大（社債価格の下落）が同時
に進みかねない点に注意が必要だ。債券を保有する場合には、表面的な利回りの高さだけでハイ
イールド債や社債ファンドを選好するのではなく、組入資産の業種の偏りを確認することや、信
用リスクの低い先進国国債などへの分散を検討することも、選択肢の一つとなる。 
第二に、株式運用において「AI関連だから買う」という一括りの発想から脱却し、企業の現金
創出力と業種分散を重視することである。NISAなどを活用してS&P500や全世界株式連動商品を保
有する投資家は多いが、これらの指数では、米国の巨大テクノロジー企業の構成比率が高まって
おり、特にS&P500では一部の企業への集中が進んでいる。指数連動商品をポートフォリオの中核
に据えつつも、補完的な資産として、安定したフリーキャッシュフローを生み出す高配当バリュ
ー株やディフェンシブ株、あるいはAIデータセンター投資の受け皿となる電力・インフラ関連株
などを組み入れることは、AI 企業の資金不足や淘汰の波に備える選択肢の一つとなるだろう。た
だし、特定のテーマや業種に偏りすぎないよう、投資比率には十分な注意が必要である。 
AI 革命は株式市場に大きな成長機会をもたらす一方で、企業の財務構造や信用市場のリスクを
も変えつつある。これからは、 「AIだから成長する」という発想にとどまらず、その裏側で膨らむ
「借金の行方」まで見据えることが重要になる。AI 企業のフリーキャッシュフローや信用スプレ
ッドの動きも、株価と同様に注視する。それが、巨額のAI投資時代において投資家に求められる
新たな視点であろう。

以 上

2 © 2026 CoreLogic NZ Limited t/as Cotality. Proprietary
Cotality’s
First Home Buyer
(FHB) Report has tracked
purchasing activity for
several years,
analysing
the
types of properties bought,
the prices paid, and broader
shifts in entry
-
level demand.
The latest edition updates
these measures and extends
the analysis to examine FHB
activity across different
price brackets.
Now in its fourth edition in
partnership with Westpac,
the report also draws on the

bank’s mortgage lending
data to provide additional
insight into borrower
characteristics. That
includes the average age of
FHB borrowers, a measure
not widely available
elsewhere, as well as loan
-
to
-
value ratios across new
mortgages.
For clarity, we define a first home
buyer purchase to be where no party
involved has owned property in New
Zealand before and are using a
mortgage to make the deal.
Note:
The Westpac FHB age and LVR data
are sourced from their lending records

and is not matched to the Cotality
database for this report.
Cotality – Westpac
First Home Buyer Report

### Page 3

3
© 2026 CoreLogic NZ Limited t/as Cotality. Proprietary
FHBs’ market share remains
at record high levels.
In Q3
2026, they accounted for
29.5% of property purchases
across NZ, surpassing the
previous mark of 28.3% only
set three months earlier. The
number of deals remains at
historically high levels too,
bucking the wider trend of
slowing sales volumes for
groups such as investors and
relocating owner
-
occupiers.
This strength for FHBs is
being seen right across the

country,
with market shares
generally running above their
own long
-
term averages
(since 2005). The wider
Wellington area remains a
hotspot for FHBs, accounting
for 37% of activity so far in
2026. But FHBs have also
been at least 30% of the
market in Hamilton and
Auckland, as well as Gisborne,
Rotorua, Hastings,
Palmerston North, and
Invercargill
–
South Waikato
and Waikato District too.
This high level of activity
hasn’t meant too many
compromises either;

standalone houses have
comprised 76% of all FHB
purchases so far in 2026, up
from 75% last year, and the
highest figure since 2020
(78%). To be fair, FHBs have
had to pay a little more, with a
median price so far this year
of $715,000, but still less than
nearly $719,000 in 2022.
FHBs are more active across
the value spectrum too.
For
example, in the middle 40% of
the market by value, their
share rose from 21% in 2015 to
28% in 2025, and now about

30% so far this year.
Access to low deposit
finance under the LVR rules
remains a key support for
first home buyers.
Recent
Reserve Bank data shows that
more than half of FHB loans
are done at less than 20%
deposit (or above 80% LVR),
and Westpac’s own lending
records show a recent
average LVR for FHBs of a
touch more than 80%.
The average age for FHBs on
Westpac’s books has ticked
higher lately, from around 35
late last year to about 35.5
now.
But rather than

necessity due to affordability
pressures, this is more likely
to reflect a choice from many
buyers to just wait for the
right property without fear the
market will run away again in
the meantime.
Looking ahead, although it’s
never easy to get that first
home, conditions should
remain
favourable
for FHBs.
Mortgage rates remain lower
than at many points in the
past, KiwiSaver access for at
least part of the deposit
remains a strong support, and
the banks remain willing and

able to lend at low
deposit/high LVR too.
This is clearly a ‘good
new
s
story’ which we should never
get blasé or complacent
about,
after so many previous
years (even decades) of
intense housing affordability
pressures and a declining
home ownership rate.
As we head into November’s
election, the bottom line is
that first home buyers are
starting from a strong base.
Key findings
Cotality – Westpac
First Home Buyer Report

### Page 4

4
© 2026 CoreLogic NZ Limited t/as Cotality. Proprietary
It’s been another solid few months
for first home buyers with their share
of house sales remaining around
record highs across the country.
That’s a stark contrast to the
softness in the broader housing
market over the past year.
Helping to support those high levels
of first home buyers have been a
couple of big trends. The first is the
large number of properties available
for sale. That’s meant those looking

to get their foot on the property
ladder have had plenty of options. As
a result, first home buyers have often
been able to get into newer and
larger standalone homes without
having to pay significantly more.
The other factor that has boosted
first home buyer numbers has been
the relatively low level of mortgage
rates in recent years. Combined with
broadly flat house prices, that’s
meant servicing a mortgage has
been much more affordable, with the

share of disposable income being
spen
t
on debt
-
servicing costs back
around pre
-
pandemic averages. For
the average first home buyer, lower
mortgage interest rates in recent
years shaved around $560 off their
monthly mortgage payments
compared to 2023 when interest
rates were at their peak. And in
Auckland, where first homes tend to
be more expensive, the saving is
around $685 a month.
While those are encouraging trends
for first home buyers, there are some

big changes in economic conditions
occurring which are likely to have
important implications for the
housing market over the year ahead.
Most notable has been the sharp rise
in inflation in the wake of the Middle
East war, with oil prices rocketing
higher since the start of this year.
That contributed to consumer prices
rising by 3.7% in the year to
September. Relatively high core
inflation levels mean we expect
inflation will push above 4% before

the end of this year and remain high
for much of 2027.
In response to those mounting
inflation pressures, and concerns
that they will persist for some time,
the RBNZ began lifting the Official
Cash Rate (OCR) back in July. We
expect further hikes in coming
months. The resulting pressure on
borrowing costs has seen mortgage
rates push higher in recent months,
with one
-
and two
-
year fixed rates
up around 0.75 percentage points
over the past year.

Even with the recent rise in
mortgage rates, the housing market
is still a lot more accessible for first
home buyers than a few years ago.
But with growing upward pressure
on interest rates, it’s a good time for
prospective first home buyers (or
anyone with a re
-
fix coming up) to
think about the best way to structure
their borrowing. That could help
insulate them from the likely trend
higher in the OCR over the next
couple of years and could save them

a lot of money over the life of their
mortgage. There’s a relatively
modest difference between short
-
and longer
-
term fixed mortgage
rates right now, which means the
cost of taking this ‘insurance’ is not
prohibitive.
Commentary from Satish Ranchhod, Westpac NZ Senior Economist

Providing some offset to the lift in
borrowing costs, house price
inflation is low, with effectively no
growth in average house prices for
three years now. And with mortgage
rates pushing higher, prices are
expected to remain soft for some
time. We expect that average house
prices will fall by 1.4% over 2026,
with a rise of just 1.8% in 2027.

At the same time, the supply of
housing is continuing to lift, with
more than 40,000 new dwellings
consented over the past year.  We
expect population growth to
increase, but from low levels, which
means the demand for housing might
not be shifting sharply higher any
time soon.
That combination of conditions
means that there is still likely to be a
lot of options
f
or
those looking to get
into their first home over the coming
year.
Cotality – Westpac

First Home Buyer Report
Source: Cotality, Westpac

### Page 6

6
© 2026 CoreLogic NZ Limited t/as Cotality. Proprietary
The national picture
Since our previous FHB Report
back in May 2026, the wider
housing market has remained
subdued and patchy
–
generally
sales volumes have been flat to
falling, while property values have
been showing a similarly soft
picture, albeit not everywhere.
Amongst the main
centres
,
Auckland and Wellington, as
examples, have been more sluggish
than say Christchurch or Dunedin.
It’s also noteworthy that the stock

of listings available for sale remains
elevated across the country.
It’s not difficult to find the reasons
why the market remains in the
doldrums.
The economic backdrop
is still uncertain, the unemployment
rate is above average, and
mortgage rates have been drifting
higher in recent months as inflation
fears remain strong. A better
balance of physical housing supply
versus our population is also
dampening prices, while the
election has been a recent reason

for caution too. It’s harder to
measure, but ‘animal spirits’ aren’t a
key factor
at the moment
either.
FHBs have gone from strength to
strength
In this purchaser
-
friendly
environment, first home buyers
have continued to take full
advantage.
The Cotality Buyer
Classification data shows that their
market share hit a new quarterly
record high of 28.3% in Q2 2026,
only to push higher still in Q3
–
reaching 29.5%. They’re also faring
well in terms of the number of deals

too, recently hovering at around
25,000 on a 12
-
month rolling basis,
back to levels last seen (for a brief
period) at the height of the post
-
COVID boom.
Clearly, lower property values
–
still down by more than 18%
nationally from the peak
–
and
improved housing affordability
(even despite some recent rises in
mortgage rates) are helping FHBs.
But those aren’t the only factors;
access to (ever
-
growing) KiwiSaver
pots for at least part of the deposit

is also a factor, alongside decent
availability of high LVR finance at
the banks.
Indeed, the 25% allowance/speed
limit to lend to owner
-
occupiers at
less than 20% equity (or >80% LVR)
is not being tested
at the moment
and recently more than half of FHB
loans have been advanced at less
than 20% deposit.
Westpac’s own
mortgage lending records show
this in a different way
–
with the
average LVR for their loans to
FHBs in recent months still
running a touch above 80%.

LVRs
tend to be higher in Auckland, but a
bit lower in Canterbury and
Wellington.
They’re also getting more for their
money
FHBs have always tended to be
willing to compromise on location
or property type to make the shift
from renting into ownership, but
lately there hasn’t been much need
to take such an approach.
Over
2026 to date (Q3), standalone
houses have comprised 76% of all
FHB purchases, up from 75% last
year, and well above 2023’s
cyclical trough of less than 70%.

For comparison, standalone houses
have accounted for around 72% of
deals across all
buyers
year to date.
N
ational
FHB activity

This shows that FHBs are getting 
the most for their money in a 
quieter market. Admittedly, the 
median price they’ve paid so far 
this year has risen to $715,000, up 
from $701,000 in 2025 
 and also  
higher than the 2023 ($695,000) 
and 2024 figures ($692,000). But 
that latest price is still around 
$4,000 lower than in 2022, yet 
FHBs are still getting ‘more house’.
Another important point to make is 
that FHBs don’t always enter at the 
bottom of the housing ladder and 
work their way up 
 – 
many 
 actually 
enter  
well above the lowest tiers of 
the market. Indeed, the FHB 
median price paid of $715,000 this 
year is below the all
 -
 buyer figure of 
$785,000, but well above the all
 -
buyer lower quartile (bottom 25%) 
of $595,000. 
This point can be made another 
way too, with Cotality figures 
showing a rising share of activity 
for FHBs right across the price 
spectrum. Perhaps most notably, 
FHBs have accounted for 30% of 
purchases in the middle 40% price 
bracket of transactions so far this 
year, up from a 28% share in 2025, 
25% in 2020, and only 21% in 2015. 
They’ve even accounted for 15% of 
activity so far this year in the top 
30% of deals split by price.
FHBs’ average age has edged 
higher again, but probably just due 
to a lack of pressure
Meanwhile, at face value, 
Westpac’s data showing that the 
average age of 
 a 
FHB has recently 
edged back up to around 2024 
levels of 35.5 again, after a trough 
of 35 late last year, could seem at 
odds with the other, more 
encouraging trends. But rather than 
being out of necessity (
 e.g.  
due to 
affordability pressures), this could 
reflect the abundance of listings 
and subdued house prices, 
meaning that 
 some/many FHBs 
might just be choosing to wait a 
bit longer to get their ideal 
property, without fear they’ll be 
left behind by a rising market.

A solid outlook
Overall, it’s never easy to buy that 
first home and FHBs certainly 
continue to face challenges. But 
their presence has remained strong 
in recent months and, although 
nothing lasts forever, it’s not hard 
to imagine that FHBs stay a solid 
presence into 2027 as well. This is 
a ‘good 
 news 
 story
 ’ which we 
should never get 
 blasé 
 or 
complacent about, after so many 
previous years (decades) of intense 
housing affordability pressures and 
a declining home ownership rate.

The main centres
Looking at the data across
Auckland, Hamilton, Tauranga,
wider Wellington (i.e. combined
City, Lower Hutt, Upper Hutt,
Porirua), Christchurch, and
Dunedin, the national trends can
also be seen at the more granular
level
–
indeed, all of these areas
have had FHB market shares lately
that are above their own averages.
The wider Wellington area remains
a standout for FHBs, where their

share so far this year has been a
touch above 37%, well
in excess of
their long
-
term average in that
market of 30%. FHBs have also had
a market share in 2026 above 30%
in Hamilton and Auckland. The
figures have been a little lower in
Christchurch and Dunedin (both
around 28%), as well as Tauranga
(circa 25%), but in each of those
markets the figures have still been
4
-
8%
-
points above their own
averages.
Looking at the different property
types, standalone houses still

dominate in most areas, accounting
for 85
-
90% of FHB purchases in
Dunedin, Tauranga, and Hamilton
–
broadly in line with the
long term
norms in those areas. The share for
houses amongst FHB purchases is
always lower in Christchurch,
Wellington, and Auckland, but it’s
perhaps the former that stands out
most at present
–
with only 66% of
FHB purchases so far in 2026 in
Christchurch being standalone
houses, versus the historical
average of 75%. This no doubt

reflects the recent townhouse
construction upswing.
In each main
centre
, the median
FHB price is lower than the figure
for all buyers, but higher than the
lower quartile for all buyers
–
in
other words, FHBs don’t always
enter at the bottom. In terms of the
prices themselves, Auckland is the
main
centre
where FHBs pay the
most, at a median of $880,000 so
far in 2026, which is $110,000 less
than the all
-
buyer median, but
$110,000 more than the all

-
buyer
lower quartile. Median FHB prices
paid are $795,500 in Tauranga so
far in 2026, followed by Wellington
at $736,250, Hamilton $715,000,
and lower still in Christchurch and
Dunedin.

The main urban areas
Digging deeper into the next 12 key
centres
around New Zealand, the
story of relative strength for FHBs’
market share has remained in place
so far in 2026. Palmerston North
stands out with 33% of
transactions in the year to date
going to FHBs (6%
-
points above its
average), although Rotorua,
Gisborne, Hastings, and Invercargill
have also had FHB shares of 30%
or more this year.  Relative to their

own averages, Napier and Hastings
have been strongest, with figures
9%
-
points above normal.
But even in areas where FHBs have
traditionally found conditions
harder or have simply been less
interested, there’s still been relative
strength this year. For example,
Whangarei’s share going to FHBs
has been 25% in 2026 (versus
average since 2005 of 19%), Kapiti
Coast at 21% versus 17%, and
Queenstown
-
Lakes at 19% versus
15%.
Standalone houses tend to be the

dominant type of property amongst
the existing dwelling stock in many
provincial areas, and therefore also
account for a high share of FHB
purchases. So far this year, houses
have been at least 90% of FHB
purchases in Invercargill,
Palmerston North, Whanganui,
Rotorua, and Gisborne. It’s been
80
-
89% in Nelson, Kapiti Coast,
Hastings, Whangarei, Napier, and
New Plymouth. Queenstown is
lower at just 64%, but that’s likely
to reflect a wider range of property

types amongst the existing stock of
dwellings, as well as affordability
pressures which are probably
pushing FHBs towards lower price
brackets (and hence smaller
dwellings).
Indeed, in terms of median prices
paid by FHBs so far this year, the
highest figure (amongst the main
urban areas) has been in
Queenstown, at $1,275,000. Kapiti
Coast comes in at $700,000, with
Whangarei, Rotorua, Napier,
Hastings, New Plymouth, and
Nelson all at least $600,000. By

contrast, Invercargill ($480,000)
and Whanganui ($460,000) are at
the lower end.

Document 407: Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground

Source PDF: all report pdf/Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to result in a material
increase in demand for bank bonds. Securitisations beneﬁt
most directly through lower capital charges, while covered
bonds could beneﬁt modestly from the new liquidity-buﬀer
rules
Maureen Schuller
On 30 January 2027, the amendments to the Solvency II regime
will apply, making these revisions one of the important regulatory
themes for 2027. One of the key objectives of the Solvency II

reforms is to release capital and promote long-term investments by
European insurance companies in support of the ambitions of
Europe’s Savings and Investments Union (SIU). Such capital release
could also increase the capacity of insurance companies to invest
more in corporate and bank bonds.
During the past decade, insurance companies have reduced their
bond holdings in favour of equity. Rather than an active
reallocation of assets, this partially reﬂects second-round eﬀects

related to the strong equity market growth and the rise in bond

yields and corresponding decline in bond valuations against the
backdrop of the ECB rate hikes in 2022 and 2023. Higher yields
have also coincided in recent years with a stronger focus by banks
on issuance in shorter maturities, rather than the longer maturities
traditionally favoured by insurance companies. This has been
particularly noteworthy for covered bonds.
Insurance companies increase equity versus
debt exposures

Source: EIOPA, ING
We don’t expect the Solvency II revisions to reverse this trend,
particularly as the amendments promote long-term equity
investments. Instead, for most bank bonds, the spread-risk capital
treatment remains broadly unchanged. Unsecured and
subordinated bank bonds continue to be treated as corporate

bonds with capital charges determined by their ratings and
duration, while qualifying covered bonds keep their preferential
treatment. The impact of the Solvency II revisions therefore arises
mainly indirectly through lower overall capital requirements, while
securitisations are the only debt instruments beneﬁting directly
through a more favourable capital treatment.
Nonetheless, certain reforms are worth highlighting for their
potential impact on insurance demand for diﬀerent bank bonds.

Among these, the amendments relating to long-term equity

### Page 3

investments, securitisations and the volatility adjustment are
particularly noteworthy.
Long-term equity investments and non-
life liquidity buffers
Under Solvency II, insurance companies can classify part of their
equity holdings as long-term equity investments (LTEI), beneﬁting
from a lower 22% capital charge. To qualify, these investments
must be held for at least ﬁve years, be listed in the EEA or OECD,
meet diversiﬁcation standards and be managed under a long-term

investment policy. The insurer must also be able to avoid forced
sales for ﬁve years, even under stressed conditions.
The Solvency II amended Delegated Regulation introduces two
approaches to demonstrate that forced equity sales can be
avoided. 1) The insurer must either have suﬃcient illiquid
homogeneous life insurance liabilities with a duration of more than
9.5 years or a liquidity buﬀer exceeding 100% for non-life insurance
obligations. 2) The insurer can also apply a forced-selling test to

prove that it can generate cash inﬂows that are consistently higher
than the cash outﬂows over ﬁve years, including under stress.
The liquidity buﬀer requirements for non-life insurance activities
are similar to the Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) requirements for
banks. The portfolio of liquid assets can comprise Level 1, Level 2A
and Level 2B assets, with the total value of Level 2A and Level 2B
assets capped at 40% and the value of the Level 2B assets at 15%.

Qualifying Level 1 assets, subject to a 0% haircut, are cash,
exposures to the ECB, multilateral development banks and
international organisations, and domestic currency exposures
to EEA central governments and central banks.
Qualifying Level 2A assets, subject to a 15% haircut, are bonds
and loans rated AA- or better that are not issued by an
insurance company or bank, and covered bonds rated AA- or
better as referred to in Article 3(1) of the European Covered

Bond Directive (CBD) (ie ‘European Covered Bonds’).
Qualifying Level 2B assets, subject to a 25% haircut, are
Simple, Transparent and Standardised (STS) securitisations (not

the relatively small share of non-life insurance obligations and
insurers’ sizeable existing portfolios of other liquid assets, such as
government bonds.

Lower capital requirements for
securitisations

### Page 5

The amended Solvency II Delegated Regulation lowers the capital
requirements for securitisation notes. This should encourage
insurers to invest more in these transactions, helping banks to
transfer risk and expand lending.
The risk factors (a and b ) for the spread risk of senior tranches
of STS securitisations will become fully aligned with the
treatment of similarly rated covered bonds as referred to in CBD
Article 3(1).
The risk factors for non-senior STS securitisations are reduced

to a similar extent, with the new capital charges for AAA-rated
non-senior notes at c.70% of the current capital charge, and for
AA-rated non-senior notes at 75% of the current requirement.
The diﬀerence in the capital treatment between STS and non-
STS securitisations is currently far more signiﬁcant for insurers
than for banks. This diﬀerence will be reduced substantially,
while the capital charge remains capped at 100%.
The current Solvency II regime makes no distinction between

senior and non-senior non-STS securitisation notes,
exaggerating the spread risk of senior compared with non-
senior tranches. Hence, lower risk factors are introduced for
senior non-STS compared to non-senior non-STS transactions.
As a result of these amendments, the diﬀerence in capital
requirements between CBD-compliant covered bonds and senior
STS securitisations will disappear from the end of January, while in
the highest rating buckets (AAA and AA) securitisations will be

treated better than unsecured bonds or third-country covered
bonds. Further down the rating scale, STS securitisations will be
treated closer to that of unsecured bonds from a capital
perspective, where corporate and bank unsecured bonds will still
beneﬁt from a more favourable capital charge than similarly rated
senior STS securitisations.
Securitisation alternatives will become
more competitive from a capital perspective

European insurance companies currently hold only about €37bn in
collateralised debt, such as asset-backed securities (ABS) and
residential mortgage-backed securities (RMBS). This is only 12% of
what they hold in covered bonds and 3% of their corporate debt
portfolio.
The Solvency II amendments may motivate insurance companies
to diversify their debt portfolios a bit further away from covered
bonds and other bank bond products towards securitisations.

However, the impact is not expected to be substantial given that
the weighted average lives of securitisation notes may not always
be well suited to the longer-term investment needs of insurance
companies.
Volatility adjustment to align spread
sensitivities more closely
Insurance companies calculate the best estimate of their liabilities
by discounting the expected future liability cash ﬂows using the
relevant risk-free interest rate term structure. To mitigate the

balance sheet impact of bond spread volatility (ie, on the asset side
of their balance sheet), insurance companies can discount their
liabilities at the risk-free rate plus a volatility adjustment (VA).
The volatility adjustment increases the
discount rate

### Page 7

Spot rate, %
Source: EIOPA, ING
For each relevant currency (or national market in the case of the
euro area), the volatility adjustment represents 65% of the spread
(S) between the interest rate on the reference portfolio and the
relevant risk-free interest rate, adjusted for a risk correction (RC). The
latter correction factor removes the part of the spread that is
attributable to expected losses or unexpected credit or other risks
to the assets. The volatility adjustment is only added to the

applicable risk-free interest rates that are not derived from
extrapolation.
For each relevant country, a country-speciﬁc adjustment may also
be added to the volatility adjustment before the application of the
65% factor if a) the risk-corrected country spread is more than twice
the risk-corrected currency spread, and b) the risk-corrected country
spread is more than 85bp. The country component in the volatility
adjustment helps mitigate excessive widening in speciﬁc countries.

Under the current regime, the representative currency and country
portfolios are constructed as uniform portfolios, even though
insurers’ actual ﬁxed-income portfolios may diﬀer substantially.
Some insurers can therefore beneﬁt excessively from the volatility
adjustment if the resulting reduction in the best estimate of their
liabilities exceeds the decline in the value of the relevant assets
when credit spreads widen.

### Page 8

For this reason, the Solvency II revisions change the calculation of
the volatility adjustment by a) making the applicable risk
corrections (RC) more spread-sensitive, and b) introducing a credit
spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR) to better align the volatility
adjustment with the insurer’s asset-liability proﬁle. The latter ratio
reﬂects the price value of a basis point of the insurer’s bond,
securitisation and loan investments versus the price value of a

basis point of the best estimate of liabilities, with a minimum of 0
and a maximum of 1.
Moreover, subject to approval by the supervisory authority, an
undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment (USA) of up to 105% may be
applied to align the risk-corrected spread on the reference portfolio
more closely with that of the insurer’s own portfolio. This must not
lead to an increase in the applied risk-adjusted spread for more
than two consecutive quarters. Moreover, when the USA is used, the

insurer cannot also increase the volatility adjustment through a
macro volatility adjustment.
The macro volatility adjustment replaces the existing country-
speciﬁc adjustment and can be added to the euro volatility
adjustment when the relevant conditions are met. It aims to
mitigate the impact of excessive spread widening in individual euro
area countries and is based on the diﬀerence between the risk-
corrected euro spread and the risk-corrected spread for the relevant

country.
Volatility adjustment calculations

The introduction of additional minimum conditions, such as
supervisory approval and the availability of adequate processes to
calculate the volatility adjustment, has also facilitated the upward

revision of the general application ratio (GAR) from 65% to 85%.
Primarily through the introduction of the credit spread sensitivity
ratio (CSSR), the volatility adjustment will better reﬂect the
relationship between each insurer’s asset and liability sensitivity to
changes in the volatility adjustment. The revision may provide
modest support for bonds that increase the asset portfolio’s spread
sensitivity, as a higher CSSR results in a larger volatility adjustment

until the ratio reaches its cap of one. The revised calculations should
also reduce the possibility for some insurers to beneﬁt
disproportionately from the volatility adjustment when their assets
are less spread-sensitive than the reference portfolio.
Conclusion
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to trigger a material
reallocation in favour of bank bonds. Securitisations are the most
obvious beneﬁciaries of a more favourable capital treatment, while

covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should mainly beneﬁt
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers. We

### Page 2

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  2

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer

Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA

Hannah Chew 
Portfolio Strategist

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk
Aspect Traditional Sukuk Sustainable Bonds Sustainable Sukuk
